<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that the Partition Principle does not imply the Axiom of Choice: if ZF is consistent, then so is ZF with the Partition Principle, Choice for ordinal-indexed families, and the negation of the Axiom of Choice. Separately, over every countable transitive model of ZFC, we construct a transitive symmetric model of this theory with the same ordinals and no new countable sequences of ground elements.

- **claim-main-consistency** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-main-consistency)): If $\mathsf{ZF}$ is consistent, then so is $\mathsf{ZF}+\mathsf{PP}+\mathsf{AC}_{\mathrm{WO}}+\neg\mathsf{AC}$.
  - Explanation: Relative consistency: from consistency of ZF one gets consistency of ZF with the Partition Principle, ordinal-indexed choice and the failure of full Choice. Syntactic consistency only; no transitive model is claimed.
- **claim-main-transitive** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main-transitive)): Let $V$ be a countable transitive model of $\mathsf{ZFC}$. There are a set forcing $\mathbb Q\in V$, a $V$-generic filter $G$, and a transitive symmetric submodel $W$ of $V[G]$ such that $$V\subseteq W\subseteq V[G],\qquad \mathrm{Ord}^W=\mathrm{Ord}^V,\qquad W\models\mathsf{ZF}+\mathsf{PP}+\mathsf{AC}_{\mathrm{WO}}+\neg\mathsf{AC}.$$ Every countable sequence of elements of $V$ that belongs to $W$ already belongs to $V$.
  - Explanation: Over a countable transitive model of ZFC there is a set forcing, a generic filter and a transitive symmetric submodel with the same ordinals that satisfies the target theory and adds no new countable sequences of ground elements.
- **claim-mono-countable-ideal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-mono-countable-ideal)): If a monoid has conditional joins and satisfies Equation <a href="#eq:mono-margin" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mono-margin">[eq:mono-margin]</a>, every ideal in $\downarrow a$ has a nondecreasing cofinal sequence of length $\omega$, with repetitions allowed. The same conclusion holds in any $\mathsf{ZFC}$ extension in which the monoid, order, heights and costs retain their meanings, for ideals belonging to that extension.
  - Explanation: With conditional joins and the height/cost margin, every ideal below an element has a countable nondecreasing cofinal sequence; the same holds in suitable extensions for ideals living there.
- **claim-monoid-construction** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-monoid)): There is a monoid $T$ of cardinality $\mu$, with a distinguished $e\ne1$, having the following properties.* 1. *$T$ is left cancellative and conical, has conditional joins, and has heights and costs satisfying Equation <a href="#eq:mono-margin" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mono-margin">[eq:mono-margin]</a>.* 2. *Every valid finite overlap prescription on copies of $T$ is realized by left translations $x\mapsto l_i x$ in $T$, with exactly the prescribed identifications.* 3. *For $a,b\in T$ with $b\ne1$, and every ideal $U\subseteq\downarrow a$, there are $\mu$ distinct elements $m$ with $$\label{eq:mono-probes} \begin{gathered} mb=ae,\qquad m\not\sqsubseteq a,\qquad \downarrow m\cap\downarrow a=U,\\ n\vee m=ae\quad(n\sqsubseteq a,\ n\notin U). \end{gathered}$$ The cones of distinct elements in this family intersect exactly in $aeT$. Moreover $\|T\setminus eT\|=\mu$.* 4. *For every countable subset of $T$ there is a countable submonoid $T_0$ containing it and a continuous conservative tower starting at $T_0$ and ending at $T$. Each successor step has one of the following properties:* 1. *no new element divides an old element;* 2. *every new element $w$ with an old right multiple has a least old right multiple $g(w)$, and $$\label{eq:mono-gate-old} d\text{ old},\quad g(w)\sqsubseteq wd \quad\Longrightarrow\quad wd\text{ old}.$$* *Here “old” means belonging to the base of that particular successor step, and the comparisons may be tested in the final monoid.
  - Explanation: Existence of the saturated diagram monoid: algebraic and margin properties, realization of valid finite overlaps, many probes with prescribed divisor profile, and countable-base towers with the gate rule.
- **claim-probe-algebra** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-mono-probe-algebra)): The presented extension $M'$ is left cancellative and conical, has conditional joins, and conservatively contains $M$. Every new element with an old right multiple has a least old right multiple, and Equation <a href="#eq:mono-gate-old" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mono-gate-old">[eq:mono-gate-old]</a> holds at this extension. For $m=p_0$, $$\label{eq:mono-probe-profile} \begin{gathered} mb=ae,\qquad m\not\sqsubseteq a,\qquad \downarrow m\cap\downarrow a=\bigcup_i\downarrow n_i,\\ x\vee m=ae\qquad (x\sqsubseteq a,\ x\not\sqsubseteq m), \end{gathered}$$ where the downsets and joins in this display are computed in $M'$.
  - Explanation: The probe extension keeps left cancellation, conicality and conditional joins, embeds the base conservatively, has gates for new elements, and realizes the prescribed divisor profile.
- **claim-probe-heights** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-mono-probe-heights)): The height and cost functions on $M$ extend to the probe extension $M'$ so that Equation <a href="#eq:mono-margin" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mono-margin">[eq:mono-margin]</a> holds. For any old comparison, its missed-cost supremum is unchanged.
  - Explanation: Height and cost functions extend to the probe extension while the margin inequality persists and old missed-cost suprema are unchanged.
- **claim-merger** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-mono-merger)): Let $M$ satisfy the algebraic and margin hypotheses, and let a finite nonempty overlap prescription on copies of $M$ be valid. There is a conservative extension realizing it by left translations. The extension is left cancellative and conical, has conditional joins, and admits extending heights and costs satisfying Equation <a href="#eq:mono-margin" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mono-margin">[eq:mono-margin]</a>. No new element divides an old one.
  - Explanation: A valid finite overlap prescription is realized by a conservative extension by left translations with all algebraic and margin properties, and without new divisors of old elements.
- **claim-simulation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-mono-simulation)): Suppose $B\subseteq M$ is conservative and both monoids have the algebraic properties above. Either extension operation with parameters in $B$ is valid over $M$, and the extension of $B$ conservatively embeds into the corresponding extension of $M$ by the same generators. A finite overlap prescription with parameters in $B$ is valid in $B$ if and only if it is valid in $M$.
  - Explanation: Both extension operations commute with conservative submonoid inclusions, and validity of a finite overlap prescription is the same over the submonoid and the monoid.
- **claim-profile-persistence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-mono-profile-persistence)): Suppose a probe satisfies Equation <a href="#eq:mono-probe-profile" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mono-probe-profile">[eq:mono-probe-profile]</a> in one stage of a tower of the two operations. It continues to satisfy that equation at every later stage and at the union, with the full later downward closure of its original path. Previously constructed joins between probes are also preserved.
  - Explanation: A probe's exact divisor profile and joins between probes persist through later stages and unions of a tower of the two operations.
- **claim-mono-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-mono-descent)): Let $(n_i)$ be a nondecreasing sequence in $T$, and choose a countable-base tower from Theorem <a href="#thm:monoid" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:monoid">4</a> whose initial submonoid contains all $n_i$ and all right quotients between them. Let $\mathcal A\subseteq\omega\times T$ be a set of pairs such that each $(l,m)\in\mathcal A$ has $m\sqsubseteq n_N$ for some $N$. Assume that for each $(l,m)\in\mathcal A$ and each $N$ there is $(l',md)\in\mathcal A$ with $l'\geq l$, $n_ld=n_{l'}$, and $n_N\sqsubseteq md$. Then, starting from any pair in $\mathcal A$, a finite succession of these replacements reaches a pair whose second coordinate belongs to the initial countable submonoid.
  - Explanation: A set of aligned pairs closed under the stated replacements reaches, in finitely many replacements, a pair whose monoid coordinate lies in the countable initial submonoid.
- **claim-cohen-properties** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cohen-properties)): The forcing $\mathbb R$ is countably closed and satisfies the $\mu$-chain condition. It adds no countable sequences of ground elements, preserves $\kappa=\aleph_1^V$, and preserves every ground cardinal at least $\mu$. In particular it preserves $\mu$ and $\mu^+$. If $V\models\mathsf{CH}$, it preserves all cardinals.
  - Explanation: The Cohen forcing is countably closed with a chain condition, adds no countable sequences of ground elements and preserves the relevant cardinals.
- **claim-diagram-amalgamation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-diagram-amalgamation)): 1. *For every $p\in\mathbb P$ and ground $J\subseteq I$ of size at most $\mu$, there is $r\in\mathbb P$ with $r_e=p$ and $J\subseteq D_r$.* 2. *A finite family $s_i\in\mathbb P$ has a common extension whenever each pair has either disjoint ranges or an intersection that is exactly the range of a common subdiagram $$s_{i,a_{ij}}=s_{j,a_{ji}}.$$* 3. *A partial placement of finitely many diagrams that is injective and consistent on their specified common parts, extends to a ground permutation of $I$. Unspecified coordinates of these diagrams and of finitely many Cohen conditions can be placed outside any prescribed ground set of size at most $\mu$.
  - Explanation: Diagrams can be extended to cover small ground sets, finite compatible families amalgamate, and partial placements extend to ground permutations.
- **claim-symmetric-model** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-symmetric-model)): The symmetric forcing relation is definable formula by formula in $V$, and it forces every axiom instance of full pure-set $\mathsf{ZF}$. If $V$ is externally transitive, the evaluated class $W$ is a transitive model of that theory, with $$V\subseteq W\subseteq V[F][H], \qquad \mathrm{Ord}^W=\mathrm{Ord}^{V[F][H]}.$$ The truth lemma holds for this evaluated model. Over an arbitrary countable choice ground, it holds for the symmetric quotient of internal names described in Section <a href="#sec:metatheory" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:metatheory">8</a>.
  - Explanation: The symmetric forcing relation is definable and forces full ZF; over an externally transitive ground the evaluated class is a transitive model with the same ordinals as the product extension.
- **claim-local-decision** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local-decision)): Fix an $\mathbb R$-generic $F$.* 1. *If $p\in\mathbb P$ and all product-name parameters $\tau_1,\ldots,\tau_k$ are supported by $D_p$, then $p$ decides each ordinary $\mathbb P$-forcing formula $\varphi(\tau_1[F],\ldots,\tau_k[F])$ in $V[F]$. The parameters may have arbitrary rank.* 2. *Suppose a Cohen condition $d$ forces that $s\Vdash_{\mathbb P}\varphi(\vec\tau[F])$, where all the product names are supported by $D_s$. Then $d\restriction(D_s\times\kappa)$ forces the same assertion.* *For brevity we write $d\restriction D_s$ for this restriction.
  - Explanation: Once the Cohen generic is fixed, a diagram decides forcing formulas whose parameters it supports, and Cohen witnesses can be restricted to the supporting diagram.
- **claim-support-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-support-descent)): Suppose $s_0,s_1\leq p$ and $D_{s_0}\cap D_{s_1}=D_p$. Let $\sigma_i$ be product $\mathrm{HS}$ names supported by $D_{s_i}$. Fix an $\mathbb R$-generic $F$. If some $v\leq s_0,s_1$ forces $\sigma_0[F]=\sigma_1[F]$ in ordinary $\mathbb P$-forcing, then there is a product $\mathrm{HS}$ name $\sigma$, supported by $D_p$, such that $$s_i\Vdash_{\mathbb P}\sigma[F]=\sigma_i[F] \qquad(i=0,1)$$ in $V[F]$. The names may have arbitrary rank. If, in $V[F]$, each $s_i$ also forces $\sigma_i[F]\subseteq A$, the descended name can be normalized to a subset name with only label names as possible members.
  - Explanation: Equality of names supported on two diagrams meeting exactly in a common subdiagram is witnessed by a name supported on that common part.
- **claim-presentation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-presentation)): In $W$, every nonempty set $X$ is the image of a surjection $A\times\eta\to X$ for some ordinal $\eta$. No bound on the rank of $X$ is imposed.
  - Explanation: Every nonempty set of the symmetric model is a surjective image of the label set times an ordinal, with no rank restriction.
- **claim-ordinal-choice** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-ordinal-choice)): The model $W$ satisfies $\mathsf{AC}_{\mathrm{WO}}$: every ordinal-indexed family of nonempty sets in $W$ has a choice function in $W$.
  - Explanation: Choice holds for ordinal-indexed families in the symmetric model.
- **claim-not-choice** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-not-choice)): The set $A$ is not wellorderable in $W$. In particular $W\models\neg\mathsf{AC}$.
  - Explanation: The label set cannot be wellordered in the symmetric model, so full Choice fails.
- **claim-no-countable-sequences** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-no-countable-sequences)): For every ground set $X$, every function $f:\omega\to X$ belonging to $W$ belongs to $V$. Consequently $W$ adds no countable sequences of ground elements.
  - Explanation: Countable sequences of ground elements in the symmetric model already lie in the ground.
- **claim-local-quotient-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-local-quotient-bound)): For every $F$ as above and every ground $r\le q$, $\|\mathcal D_F(r)\|\le\mu$ in $V[F]$.
  - Explanation: The set of local symbolic classes at a diagram has size at most the cardinal parameter.
- **claim-availability-ideals** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-availability-ideals)): Each set in Equations <a href="#eq:class-availability" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:class-availability">[eq:class-availability]</a> and <a href="#eq:label-availability" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:label-availability">[eq:label-availability]</a> is an ideal of $\downarrow a$. Every ideal of $\downarrow a$ in $V[F]$ belongs to $V$.
  - Explanation: Availability sets of classes and labels are ideals, and every such ideal of the forcing extension already lies in the ground.
- **claim-exact-fibers** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-exact-fibers)): For every $r\le q$ and every nonempty ideal $U\subseteq\downarrow a$, where $q=r_a$, both $B_{r,U}(F)$ and $L_{r,U}$ have cardinality $\mu$ in $V[F]$. The label fiber already has that cardinality in $V$.
  - Explanation: For each nonempty ideal the exact class fiber and the exact label fiber both have the full cardinal parameter as size.
- **claim-contraction-fibers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-contraction-fibers)): For every contraction $v\to w$, the label fibers satisfy $L_v=L_w$, and inclusion of symbolic elements gives a bijection $B_w(F)\longrightarrow B_v(F)$. These identifications commute with composition.
  - Explanation: Contractions preserve label fibers and give compatible bijections between symbolic fibers.
- **claim-countable-germs** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-countable-germs)): In the ground model, $\|G_C/K_C\|\le\aleph_0$.
  - Explanation: The component stabilizer modulo the tail fixers is countable in the ground model.
- **claim-least-code-covariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-least-code-covariance)): For every $k\in K_C$, transport $T_k^F$ is an order isomorphism from $(B_C(F),<_F)$ to $(B_C(kF),<_{kF})$. The group $K_C$ fixes $L_C$ pointwise.
  - Explanation: Tail fixers transport the least-code wellorder isomorphically and fix the labels pointwise.
- **claim-coset-selector** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-coset-selector)): Formula <a href="#eq:detector-pattern" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:detector-pattern">[eq:detector-pattern]</a> is independent of the representative of $\pi K_C$. For each $F$ there is an $\alpha<\kappa$ at which all distinct cosets have distinct patterns. There is a definable selection $c_C(F)\in\Gamma_C$ satisfying $$\label{eq:coset-selector-covariance} c_C(\sigma F)=\sigma c_C(F)\qquad(\sigma\in G_C).$$
  - Explanation: The Cohen-row pattern is independent of the coset representative, separates cosets at some bounded level, and yields a covariant selection of a coset.
- **claim-quotient-classification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-quotient-classification)): In $W$, every nonwellorderable surjective image of $A$ is in bijection with $A$.
  - Explanation: Every nonwellorderable surjective image of the label set is in bijection with it inside the symmetric model, via one hereditarily symmetric graph.
- **claim-pp-reduction** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lemma-pp-reduction)): Work in $\mathsf{ZF}$. Suppose that a nonempty set $A$ has the following properties.* 1. *For every nonempty set $X$, there are an ordinal $\eta$ and a surjection $A\times\eta\longrightarrow X$.* 2. *Every ordinal-indexed family of nonempty sets has a choice function.* 3. *Every nonwellorderable surjective image of $A$ is in bijection with $A$.* *Then $\mathsf{PP}$ holds: for all sets $X,Y$, every surjection $f:X\longrightarrow Y$ has an injection $j:Y\longrightarrow X$.
  - Explanation: In ZF, a presentation property, ordinal-indexed choice and bijectivity of nonwellorderable images with the set A together give the Partition Principle.
- **claim-explicit-choice-failure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-explicit-choice-failure)): In $\mathsf{ZF}$, if a set $A$ is not wellorderable, then $\mathcal P(A)\setminus\{\varnothing\}$ has no choice function.
  - Explanation: For a nonwellorderable set, the family of its nonempty subsets has no choice function, shown by a Hartogs recursion.
- **claim-construction-summary** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-construction-summary)): Let $V$ be a ground model of $\mathsf{ZFC}$, put $$\kappa=\omega_1^V,\qquad \mu=\bigl((2^{\aleph_0})^+\bigr)^V, \qquad \|I\|^V=\mu^+,$$ and use the monoid of Theorem <a href="#thm:monoid" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:monoid">4</a> and the symmetric system constructed above. In an ordinary generic extension for its product forcing, the resulting symmetric model $W$ satisfies $$W\models\mathsf{ZF}+\mathsf{PP}+\mathsf{AC}_{\mathrm{WO}}+\neg\mathsf{AC}.$$ It contains $V$ and has the same ordinals as $V$. It adds no countable sequences of ground elements: if $b\in V$ and $z\in W$ is a function $\omega\longrightarrow b$, then $z\in V$. The set of labels $A$ is not wellorderable in $W$, and $$\mathcal P^W(A)\setminus\{\varnothing\}$$ is an explicit family in $W$ with no choice function in $W$.
  - Explanation: Summary of the construction over a ground model of ZFC: the symmetric model satisfies the target theory, preserves ordinals and countable ground sequences, and the nonempty subsets of the label set form a family without a choice function.
- **claim-constructible-consistency** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-constructible-consistency)): For every axiom $\alpha$ of $\mathsf{ZFC}+V=L$, its relativization $\alpha^L$ is provable in $\mathsf{ZF}$. Consequently $$\operatorname{Con}(\mathsf{ZF})\ \Longrightarrow\ \operatorname{Con}(\mathsf{ZFC}+V=L).$$ Moreover, $\mathsf{ZFC}+V=L$ proves the generalized continuum hypothesis.
  - Explanation: Relativization to the constructible universe turns consistency of ZF into consistency of ZFC with V=L, which proves GCH.
- **claim-symmetric-forcing-schema** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-symmetric-forcing-schema)): For each fixed formula $\varphi$ in the language of set theory, the relation $$p\Vdash_{\mathrm{HS}_{\mathcal S}}\varphi(\tau_1,\ldots,\tau_n)$$ is definable in $\mathsf{ZFC}$, with $\mathcal S$ as a parameter and $\tau_1,\ldots,\tau_n\in\mathrm{HS}_{\mathcal S}$. It satisfies the forcing rules for first-order logic with equality and the symmetry lemma. For each axiom $\alpha$ of $\mathsf{ZF}$, including each Separation and Replacement instance, $\mathsf{ZFC}$ proves $$\mathcal S\text{ is a set symmetric system} \quad\Longrightarrow\quad 1\Vdash_{\mathrm{HS}_{\mathcal S}}\alpha.$$
  - Explanation: Symmetric forcing is a first-order definable schema forcing every ZF axiom instance, including Separation and Replacement for names of arbitrary rank.
- **claim-arbitrary-model-truth** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-arbitrary-model-symmetric-truth)): For each standard first-order formula $\varphi$ and internal hereditarily symmetric names $\vec\tau$, $$W\models\varphi([\vec\tau]_G) \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad (\exists p\in G)\ M\models p\Vdash_{\mathrm{HS}_{\mathcal S}}\varphi(\vec\tau).$$ In particular $W\models\mathsf{ZF}$.
  - Explanation: Truth in the symmetric quotient of a countable, possibly ill-founded model is given by forcing in the model; hence the quotient satisfies ZF.

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-diagram-amalgamation",
        "exact_quote": "1.  *For every $p\\in\\mathbb P$ and ground $J\\subseteq I$ of size at most $\\mu$, there is $r\\in\\mathbb P$ with $r_e=p$ and $J\\subseteq D_r$.*\n\n2.  *A finite family $s_i\\in\\mathbb P$ has a common extension whenever each pair has either disjoint ranges or an intersection that is exactly the range of a common subdiagram $$s_{i,a_{ij}}=s_{j,a_{ji}}.$$*\n\n3.  *A partial placement of finitely many diagrams that is injective and consistent on their specified common parts, extends to a ground permutation of $I$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-symmetric-model",
        "exact_quote": "The symmetric forcing relation is definable formula by formula in $V$, and it forces every axiom instance of full pure-set $\\mathsf{ZF}$. If $V$ is externally transitive, the evaluated class $W$ is a transitive model of that theory, with $$V\\subseteq W\\subseteq V[F][H],\n \\qquad \\mathrm{Ord}^W=\\mathrm{Ord}^{V[F][H]}.$$ The truth lemma holds for this evaluated model. Over an arbitrary countable choice ground, it holds for the symmetric quotient of internal names described in Section <a href=\"#sec:metatheory\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:metatheory\">8</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-244.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Fix an $\\mathbb R$-generic $F$.*\n\n1.  *If $p\\in\\mathbb P$ and all product-name parameters $\\tau_1,\\ldots,\\tau_k$ are supported by $D_p$, then $p$ decides each ordinary $\\mathbb P$-forcing formula $\\varphi(\\tau_1[F],\\ldots,\\tau_k[F])$ in $V[F]$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-support-descent",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $s_0,s_1\\leq p$ and $D_{s_0}\\cap D_{s_1}=D_p$. Let $\\sigma_i$ be product $\\mathrm{HS}$ names supported by $D_{s_i}$. Fix an $\\mathbb R$-generic $F$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lemma-pp-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "Work in $\\mathsf{ZF}$. Suppose that a nonempty set $A$ has the following properties.*\n\n1.  *For every nonempty set $X$, there are an ordinal $\\eta$ and a surjection $A\\times\\eta\\longrightarrow X$.*\n\n2.  *Every ordinal-indexed family of nonempty sets has a choice function.*\n\n3.  *Every nonwellorderable surjective image of $A$ is in bijection with $A$.*\n\n*Then $\\mathsf{PP}$ holds: for all sets $X,Y$, every surjection $f:X\\longrightarrow Y$ has an injection $j:Y\\longrightarrow X$.",
        "source_id": "PM-244.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lemma-pp-reduction",
          "exact_quote": "Work in $\\mathsf{ZF}$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Partition Principle (equivalent form)** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Equivalently, every partition of a set admits an injection into that set.
- **AC_WO** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Write $\mathsf{AC}_{\mathrm{WO}}$ for Choice for wellorderable families: for every ordinal $\eta$ and every family $(X_\xi)_{\xi<\eta}$ of nonempty sets, there is a function $c$ with $c(\xi)\in X_\xi$ for all $\xi<\eta$.
- **SVC(A)** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For a nonempty set $A$, $\mathrm{SVC}(A)$ says that every nonempty set is a surjective image of $A\times\eta$ for some ordinal $\eta$.
- **Divisibility order** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Write $x\sqsubseteq y$ if $xz=y$ for some $z$.
- **Conical monoid** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A monoid is *conical* if $xy=1$ implies $x=y=1$.
- **Conditional join** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A *conditional join* is an element $x\vee y$ satisfying $$xM\cap yM=(x\vee y)M$$ whenever the intersection is nonempty.
- **Ideal in a principal downset** ([source](#pm-h-6)): An *ideal* in $\downarrow a$ will mean a nonempty downward closed subset closed under finite nonempty joins.
- **Conservative inclusion** ([source](#pm-h-6)): An inclusion $B\subseteq M$ is *conservative* if it preserves and reflects comparisons, preserves their right factors, preserves existing joins, and preserves the absence of a common right multiple.
- **Valid overlap prescription** ([source](#pm-h-6)): We call the prescription *valid* if these pair identifications, together with equality, already form an equivalence relation and do not identify distinct elements of any one copy.
- **Cohen forcing conditions** ([source](#pm-h-13)): Here a condition of $\mathbb R$ is a partial function $I\times\kappa\to2$ with countable domain, ordered by reverse inclusion.
- **Permutation group G** ([source](#pm-h-14)): Let $G=\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}(I)^V$.
- **Support of a name** ([source](#pm-h-14)): A name has support $J$ when $\mathop{\mathrm{Fix}}(J)$ fixes it literally.
- **Hereditarily symmetric name** ([source](#pm-h-14)): A hereditarily symmetric name, abbreviated $\mathrm{HS}$, has a support and only hereditarily symmetric member names.
- **Vertex** ([source](#pm-h-23)): A vertex is a pair $v=(r,U)$, where $r\le q$, $q=r_a$, and $U$ is a nonempty ideal of $\downarrow a$.
- **Component** ([source](#pm-h-23)): $$ Write $z\vee n=nx$. Availability at $r_{z\vee n}$ is exactly the corresponding availability at the subdiagram $(r_n)_x$ of $r_n$. Thus the restricted ideal at $r_n$ determines the full ideal at $r$. If that restricted ideal is $U_n$, the last test is equivalent to $z\vee n\in U$, and hence to $z\in U$. Conversely, an element of $B_v(F)$ is available at $r_n$ because $n\in U$, and its restricted ideal is $U_n$. This proves surjectivity; injectivity follows from Equation <a href="#eq:symbolic-inclusion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:symbolic-inclusion">[eq:symbolic-inclusion]</a>. For labels use the same argument with literal membership in ranges. Commutation follows because all the symbolic maps retain the same representative names. ◻ </details> Let a component be a connected component of the vertex system when the direction of contractions is ignored.
- **Symmetric system** ([source](#pm-h-35)): Write $\mathcal S=(\mathbb Q,\Gamma,\mathcal F)$ for a set symmetric system: $\mathbb Q$ is a nonempty set poset, $\Gamma$ is a set group of automorphisms of $\mathbb Q$, and $\mathcal F$ is a normal filter of subgroups of $\Gamma$.

      "exact_definition": "$$ Write $z\\vee n=nx$. Availability at $r_{z\\vee n}$ is exactly the corresponding availability at the subdiagram $(r_n)_x$ of $r_n$. Thus the restricted ideal at $r_n$ determines the full ideal at $r$. If that restricted ideal is $U_n$, the last test is equivalent to $z\\vee n\\in U$, and hence to $z\\in U$. Conversely, an element of $B_v(F)$ is available at $r_n$ because $n\\in U$, and its restricted ideal is $U_n$. This proves surjectivity; injectivity follows from Equation <a href=\"#eq:symbolic-inclusion\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:symbolic-inclusion\">[eq:symbolic-inclusion]</a>. For labels use the same argument with literal membership in ranges. Commutation follows because all the symbolic maps retain the same representative names. ◻ </details> Let a component be a connected component of the vertex system when the direction of contractions is ignored.",
      "interpretation": "Connected pieces of the vertex system on which one coherent bijection is built.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-countable-ideal** [claim](#stmt-lem-mono-countable-ideal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mono-countable-ideal): Choose elements with heights approaching the supremum, replace by finite joins, and use the margin to exclude missed divisors. -> countable cofinal sequence in an ideal
- **step-probe-algebra** [claim](#stmt-lem-mono-probe-algebra) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mono-probe-algebra): Normal forms via a direct limit and rewriting, gate analysis of exits, then joins and divisor profile. -> algebraic properties and divisor profile of the probe extension
- **step-probe-heights** [claim](#stmt-lem-mono-probe-heights) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mono-probe-heights): Place each first-letter block in a short height interval and define heights and costs recursively. -> margin-preserving heights and costs
- **step-merger** [claim](#stmt-lem-mono-merger) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mono-merger): Form the quotient of copies, adjoin translation generators, read cones from the final letter, average heights. -> conservative merger extension
- **step-simulation** [claim](#stmt-lem-mono-simulation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mono-simulation): Compare direct limits and merger quotients over a conservative submonoid. -> conservative simulation of both operations
- **step-profile-persistence** [claim](#stmt-lem-mono-profile-persistence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mono-profile-persistence): Follow gates of new divisors at probe steps; mergers add no divisors. -> persistent probe profile
- **step-monoid** [claim](#stmt-thm-monoid) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mono-profile-persistence-2): Saturating rounds of probes and mergers, then an elementary-submodel reordering for the countable base. -> the diagram monoid
- **step-mono-descent** [claim](#stmt-lem-mono-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mono-descent): Descent by birth ordinals using the gate rule. -> replacement reaching the countable base
- **step-cohen** [claim](#stmt-lem-cohen-properties) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cohen-properties): Countable closure and delta-system chain condition argument. -> properties of the Cohen forcing
- **step-amalgamation** [claim](#stmt-lem-diagram-amalgamation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-diagram-amalgamation): Probes give many positions off a cone; merger gives amalgamation; permutations extend partial bijections. -> extension and amalgamation of diagrams
- **step-symmetric-model** [claim](#stmt-thm-symmetric-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-symmetric-model): Apply the symmetric-extension theorem to the product forcing, group and normal filter. -> symmetric model of ZF
- **step-local-decision** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-decision) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-decision): Move contrary witnesses apart by a permutation fixing the support and use product symmetry. -> decision over a supporting diagram
- **step-support-descent** [claim](#stmt-lem-support-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-support-descent): Coherence of active copies via a third independent copy, then a mixture name supported on the root. -> descended name
- **step-presentation** [claim](#stmt-prop-presentation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-presentation): Orbit maps of names give partial functions on labels whose ranges cover the set. -> surjection from labels times an ordinal
- **step-ordinal-choice** [claim](#stmt-prop-ordinal-choice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-ordinal-choice): Probe density yields a wellordered slice meeting every member; reduce general families by presentation. -> choice for ordinal-indexed families
- **step-not-choice** [claim](#stmt-prop-not-choice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-not-choice): Move a decided coordinate by a permutation fixing the support. -> labels not wellorderable
- **step-no-countable** [claim](#stmt-prop-no-countable-sequences) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-no-countable-sequences): Decide values by one diagram, form the sequence in the Cohen extension, use countable closure. -> no new countable sequences
- **step-local-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-quotient-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-quotient-bound): Record triples and compare by a permutation fixing the root. -> size bound on local classes
- **step-availability** [claim](#stmt-lem-availability-ideals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-availability-ideals): Support descent over the join and countable cofinality. -> availability sets are ideals
- **step-exact-fibers** [claim](#stmt-lem-exact-fibers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exact-fibers): Find a class unavailable at the root, copy it along probes, and apply support descent. -> exact fibers of full size
- **step-contraction** [claim](#stmt-lem-contraction-fibers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-contraction-fibers): Restricted ideal at the contracted diagram determines the full ideal. -> compatible fiber identifications
- **step-countable-germs** [claim](#stmt-lem-countable-germs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-countable-germs): Apply the descent lemma to alignments of a permutation with the chain. -> countable stabilizer quotient
- **step-least-code** [claim](#stmt-lem-least-code-covariance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-least-code-covariance): Tail fixers fix every code entry eventually. -> covariant least-code order
- **step-coset-selector** [claim](#stmt-lem-coset-selector) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coset-selector): Cohen row patterns modulo eventual equality separate cosets below a regular cardinal. -> covariant coset selection
- **step-quotient-classification** [claim](#stmt-thm-quotient-classification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-quotient-classification): Collect local assignments into one hereditarily symmetric graph name. -> bijection of quotients with the label set
- **step-pp-reduction** [claim](#stmt-lemma-pp-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-pp-reduction): Slice the target into ordinal-indexed pieces and assemble piecewise injections by ordinal choice. -> Partition Principle in ZF from three hypotheses
- **step-explicit-failure** [claim](#stmt-lemma-explicit-choice-failure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-explicit-choice-failure): Hartogs recursion with a sentinel. -> family without choice function
- **step-construction-summary** [claim](#stmt-thm-construction-summary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-construction-summary): Combine the symmetric model theorem, presentation, ordinal choice, quotient classification and reduction. -> summary theorem
- **step-constructible** [claim](#stmt-lem-constructible-consistency) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-constructible-consistency): Relativize axioms to the constructible universe. -> consistency of ZFC plus V=L from ZF
- **step-forcing-schema** [claim](#stmt-lem-symmetric-forcing-schema) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-symmetric-forcing-schema): Define forcing by recursion and verify each axiom schema with set-sized name bounds. -> symmetric forcing schema
- **step-arbitrary-truth** [claim](#stmt-lem-arbitrary-model-symmetric-truth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-arbitrary-model-symmetric-truth): Induction on standard formulas over the quotient of internal names. -> truth lemma for the symmetric quotient
- **step-main-consistency** [claim](#stmt-thm-main-consistency) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-arbitrary-model-symmetric-truth-2): Countable model of ZFC plus V=L, generic filter, symmetric quotient, soundness. -> relative consistency
- **step-main-transitive** [claim](#stmt-thm-main-transitive) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-arbitrary-model-symmetric-truth-3): Run the construction in the transitive ground, choose a generic by external recursion, bound ranks of sequences. -> transitive symmetric submodel

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-relative-consistency**: Consistency of ZF transfers to consistency of ZF together with the Partition Principle, Choice for ordinal-indexed families, and the failure of full Choice.
- **inv-transitive-model**: Over a countable transitive model of ZFC there is a transitive symmetric submodel with the same ordinals satisfying the same theory, and every countable sequence of ground elements lying in it already lies in the ground.
- **inv-label-set-not-wellorderable**: The set of generic labels cannot be wellordered in the symmetric model, so full Choice fails there.
- **inv-quotient-classification**: Inside the symmetric model every nonwellorderable surjective image of the label set is in bijection with the label set.
- **inv-all-set-reduction**: In ZF, a presentation of every set as an image of the label set times an ordinal, ordinal-indexed choice, and the bijection property of nonwellorderable images together give the Partition Principle for all sets.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-label-wellordering**: Treat the label set as if an algorithmic enumeration of it existed and use it to build a global choice or inversion routine. -> The label set has no wellordering in the model, so no enumeration-based selection exists.
- **anti-transitive-from-consistency**: Read the relative consistency theorem as producing a transitive model from mere syntactic consistency. -> A transitive symmetric model is only obtained from a transitive ground model.
- **anti-choice-over-nonwellorderable**: Assume a choice function exists for the nonempty subsets of any set. -> For a nonwellorderable set the family of its nonempty subsets has no choice function.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are relative-consistency and forcing statements about infinite, nonwellorderable structures: the symmetric model is built from generic filters over an uncountable product forcing, and the label set admits no wellordering, so no finite procedure computes any of the stated objects.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves relative-consistency statements about models of set theory. It gives no program, interface or library behaviour that code could reproduce. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The symmetric model is built from generic filters over uncountable forcings and cannot be stepped or sampled in a simulation. Nothing in the source describes a dynamics or an agent model. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No tool, checker or generator is proposed. The only constructive pieces are infinite or transfinite, so no practical utility can be extracted from them. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The reverse injections are shown to exist inside the symmetric model through forcing arguments. No finite procedure computes them, and the source states no running time. The wellorderable case only uses Choice over ordinal-indexed families. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The diagram monoid is a conical left-cancellative monoid of infinite size that serves as an index set for forcing conditions. It is not a storable or queryable structure with operations to implement. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the source concerns circuits, memory hierarchies or parallel execution. The objects are sets of infinite cardinality with no finite hardware realisation. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, adversary or protocol appears. Countable closure of the forcing concerns sequences of ground elements, not secrecy or randomness. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no data, loss, model class or training procedure. Its surjection and injection statements concern abstract sets, not learned maps. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective, constraint set or solution method occurs. The family without a choice function is a pure existence statement that no solver could be asked to optimise. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There are no nodes, messages, failures or consensus requirements. The symmetric group acting on labels is a device for names in forcing, not a replication or coordination model. |
| other (formal verification and foundations of mathematics) | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The result fixes which choice-free reasoning principles are unprovable to separate from Choice: a formal development may assume the Partition Principle and Choice for ordinal-indexed families without gaining full Choice. The usable content is a reduction lemma that is stated and proved in ZF, so a proof checker can assume its three hypotheses and derive the Partition Principle. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-zf-consistency-hypothesis** (hypothesis): The principal theorem is conditional: it yields consistency of the target theory only from consistency of ZF, and it gives a syntactic relative consistency result, not a model of full Choice. Blocked use: ['Treating the theorem as an unconditional existence statement for a model of the target theory.', 'Reading it as showing that full Choice holds anywhere.']
- **limit-transitive-ground** (hypothesis): The transitive symmetric submodel is obtained only when the ground is a countable transitive model of ZFC. The consistency theorem permits externally ill-founded models and gives no transitive one. Blocked use: ['Deducing a transitive model from mere consistency of ZF.', 'Using the transitive-model conclusion over an uncountable or non-transitive ground.']
- **limit-nonwellorderable-labels** (counterexample_scope): The label set is not wellorderable in the symmetric model, so no enumeration-based global selection exists there. Only ordinal-indexed choice is available. Blocked use: ['Building a global choice function or an effective inversion routine from the label set.', 'Assuming a choice function for nonempty subsets of arbitrary sets.']
- **limit-reduction-hypotheses** (formal_scope): The all-set reduction works in ZF and needs all three hypotheses on the set A: the surjective presentation, ordinal-indexed choice, and bijectivity of nonwellorderable images with A. It does not respect the fibers of the original surjection. Blocked use: ['Applying the reduction when one of the three hypotheses is dropped.', 'Expecting the produced injection to be a section of the given surjection.']
- **limit-nonconstructive-forcing** (nonconstructive): The model is built from generic filters over an uncountable product forcing, and several steps use Choice in the ordinary extension or a countable elementary submodel. Nothing is computed. Blocked use: ['Extracting an algorithm or complexity bound from the construction.', 'Using the construction as a runnable procedure.']
- **limit-cardinal-preservation** (edge_case): The Cohen forcing is claimed to preserve only the first uncountable cardinal and the ground cardinals at least mu. No preservation of cardinals strictly between them is asserted in a general ground. Blocked use: ['Assuming all cardinals strictly between the two preserved ones survive.', 'Recomputing mu from a new continuum in the extension.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 244 (`lean/docs/244.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [The Partition Principle does not imply Choice](../../preprints/The-Partition-Principle-does-not-imply-Choice-September-24-2026/partition-principle-without-choice.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The Partition Principle says that every surjection admits an injection in the reverse direction. The formalization proves the relative-consistency implication: if ZF is consistent, then so is ZF with the Partition Principle, Choice for ordinal-indexed families, and failure of the Axiom of Choice.
>
> The linked model-construction result also constructs a transitive model of the Partition Principle without Choice from an externally countable transitive ground model satisfying the stated axioms, including Choice, and containing an internal strongly inaccessible cardinal. The paper's stronger transitive-model preservation assertions are outside that selected construction.

Comparator source `PartitionConsistency.lean` (14862 bytes, sha256 `7b68f2154b044771ced4c3335f58a31e2070abe27ba600d947ebfd82958eb4ae`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace PartitionConsistency

/-- Pure first-order membership syntax with equality. `n` variables are in scope.
A quantifier binds the last variable in its body. There are no atoms/urelements,
function symbols, constants, semantic predicates, or mathematical assumptions
in this syntax. -/
inductive Formula : ℕ → Type where
  | bot {n} : Formula n
  | mem {n} (x y : Fin n) : Formula n
  | equal {n} (x y : Fin n) : Formula n
  | imp {n} (p q : Formula n) : Formula n
  | conj {n} (p q : Formula n) : Formula n
  | disj {n} (p q : Formula n) : Formula n
  | all {n} (p : Formula (n + 1)) : Formula n
  | ex {n} (p : Formula (n + 1)) : Formula n

namespace Formula

abbrev neg {n} (p : Formula n) : Formula n := p.imp .bot
abbrev iff {n} (p q : Formula n) : Formula n := (p.imp q).conj (q.imp p)

/-- Under a binder, a renaming leaves the newly bound variable bound. -/
def liftRen {n m} (r : Fin n → Fin m) : Fin (n + 1) → Fin (m + 1) :=
  Fin.lastCases (Fin.last m) (fun i => (r i).castSucc)

/-- Capture-avoiding renaming; in this relational language all terms are variables. -/
def rename {n m} (r : Fin n → Fin m) : Formula n → Formula m
  | .bot => .bot
  | .mem x y => .mem (r x) (r y)
  | .equal x y => .equal (r x) (r y)
  | .imp p q => .imp (rename r p) (rename r q)
  | .conj p q => .conj (rename r p) (rename r q)
  | .disj p q => .disj (rename r p) (rename r q)
  | .all p => .all (rename (liftRen r) p)
  | .ex p => .ex (rename (liftRen r) p)

abbrev raise {n} (p : Formula n) : Formula (n + 1) := rename Fin.castSucc p

/-- Substitution of a variable term for the variable just quantified. -/
def instantiate {n} (p : Formula (n + 1)) (x : Fin n) : Formula n :=
  rename (Fin.lastCases x id) p

/-- Universal closure of all the free variables. -/
def close : {n : ℕ} → Formula n → Formula 0
  | 0, p => p
  | _ + 1, p => close (.all p)

end Formula

abbrev Sentence := Formula 0
abbrev Theory := Set Sentence

/-- Classical first-order natural deduction with equality. Hypotheses may be an
infinite theory, but a derivation is a finite inductive proof. The `allI` and
`exE` rules use a fresh last variable, and shift all assumptions to avoid
capture. We allow finitely many free variables in proofs of closed sentences. -/
inductive Derives : {n : ℕ} → Set (Formula n) → Formula n → Prop where
  | hyp {n} {Γ : Set (Formula n)} {p} : p ∈ Γ → Derives Γ p
  | weaken {n} {Γ Δ : Set (Formula n)} {p} :
      Γ ⊆ Δ → Derives Γ p → Derives Δ p
  | impI {n} {Γ : Set (Formula n)} {p q} :
      Derives (insert p Γ) q → Derives Γ (.imp p q)
  | impE {n} {Γ : Set (Formula n)} {p q} :
      Derives Γ (.imp p q) → Derives Γ p → Derives Γ q
  | botE {n} {Γ : Set (Formula n)} {p} : Derives Γ .bot → Derives Γ p
  | classical {n} {Γ : Set (Formula n)} {p} :
      Derives (insert p.neg Γ) .bot → Derives Γ p
  | conjI {n} {Γ : Set (Formula n)} {p q} :
      Derives Γ p → Derives Γ q → Derives Γ (.conj p q)
  | conjL {n} {Γ : Set (Formula n)} {p q} :
      Derives Γ (.conj p q) → Derives Γ p
  | conjR {n} {Γ : Set (Formula n)} {p q} :
      Derives Γ (.conj p q) → Derives Γ q
  | disjL {n} {Γ : Set (Formula n)} {p q} :
      Derives Γ p → Derives Γ (.disj p q)
  | disjR {n} {Γ : Set (Formula n)} {p q} :
      Derives Γ q → Derives Γ (.disj p q)
  | disjE {n} {Γ : Set (Formula n)} {p q r} :
      Derives Γ (.disj p q) → Derives (insert p Γ) r →
      Derives (insert q Γ) r → Derives Γ r
  | allI {n} {Γ : Set (Formula n)} {p : Formula (n + 1)} :
      Derives (Formula.raise '' Γ) p → Derives Γ (.all p)
  | allE {n} {Γ : Set (Formula n)} {p : Formula (n + 1)} (t : Fin n) :
      Derives Γ (.all p) → Derives Γ (p.instantiate t)
  | exI {n} {Γ : Set (Formula n)} {p : Formula (n + 1)} (t : Fin n) :
      Derives Γ (p.instantiate t) → Derives Γ (.ex p)
  | exE {n} {Γ : Set (Formula n)} {p : Formula (n + 1)} {q : Formula n} :
      Derives Γ (.ex p) → Derives (insert p (Formula.raise '' Γ)) q.raise →
      Derives Γ q
  | eqRefl {n} {Γ : Set (Formula n)} (t : Fin n) : Derives Γ (.equal t t)
  | eqSubst {n} {Γ : Set (Formula n)} {s t : Fin n} (p : Formula (n + 1)) :
      Derives Γ (.equal s t) → Derives Γ (p.instantiate s) →
      Derives Γ (p.instantiate t)
  | rename {n m} {Γ : Set (Formula n)} {p : Formula n} (r : Fin n → Fin m) :
      Derives Γ p → Derives (Formula.rename r '' Γ) (Formula.rename r p)

/-- Sentences are usable as open hypotheses with any finite supply of variables. -/
def Theory.at (T : Theory) (n : ℕ) : Set (Formula n) :=
  Formula.rename Fin.elim0 '' T

/-- Ordinary syntactic consistency: there is no finite natural-deduction proof
of falsity from the theory, with any finite supply of free variables. -/
def Consistent (T : Theory) : Prop := ∀ n, ¬ Derives (T.at n) .bot

namespace SetLanguage
open Formula

/-! All abbreviations in this namespace expand to formulas of the pure language
of equality and membership. Functions are sets of Kuratowski pairs *inside the
structure*. In particular, no quantification over external Lean functions is
used in PP, Choice, or ordinal-indexed Choice. -/

/-- `a ⊆ b`. -/
def subset {n} (a b : Fin n) : Formula n :=
  rename ![a, b] (Formula.all ((Formula.mem 2 0).imp (Formula.mem 2 1)) : Formula 2)

/-- `a = ∅`. -/
def empty {n} (a : Fin n) : Formula n :=
  rename ![a] (Formula.all (Formula.mem 1 0).neg : Formula 1)

/-- `a ≠ ∅`, stated as existence of a member. -/
def nonempty {n} (a : Fin n) : Formula n :=
  rename ![a] (Formula.ex (Formula.mem 1 0) : Formula 1)

/-- `p = {x,y}`. -/
def unorderedPair {n} (p x y : Fin n) : Formula n :=
  rename ![p, x, y]
    (Formula.all ((Formula.mem 3 0).iff ((Formula.equal 3 1).disj (Formula.equal 3 2))) : Formula 3)

/-- `b = a ∪ {a}`. -/
def successor {n} (b a : Fin n) : Formula n :=
  rename ![b, a]
    (Formula.all ((Formula.mem 2 0).iff ((Formula.mem 2 1).disj (Formula.equal 2 1))) : Formula 2)

/-- Kuratowski ordered pair: `p = {{x},{x,y}}`. -/
def orderedPair {n} (p x y : Fin n) : Formula n :=
  rename ![p, x, y]
    (Formula.ex (Formula.ex ((unorderedPair 3 1 1).conj
      ((unorderedPair 4 1 2).conj (unorderedPair 0 3 4)))) : Formula 3)

/-- The internal graph relation `⟨x,y⟩ ∈ f`. -/
def edge {n} (f x y : Fin n) : Formula n :=
  rename ![f, x, y]
    (Formula.ex ((Formula.mem 3 0).conj (orderedPair 3 1 2)) : Formula 3)

/-- `f` is a set graph of a function whose domain is exactly `d`.
The first conjunct excludes non-pairs and graph entries outside the domain;
the second conjunct asserts totality and single-valuedness. -/
def functionOn {n} (f d : Fin n) : Formula n :=
  rename ![f, d]
    ((Formula.all ((Formula.mem 2 0).imp
        (Formula.ex (Formula.ex ((Formula.mem 3 1).conj (orderedPair 2 3 4)))))).conj
      (Formula.all ((Formula.mem 2 1).imp
        (Formula.ex ((edge 0 2 3).conj (Formula.all ((edge 0 2 4).imp (Formula.equal 4 3))))))) : Formula 2)

/-- Internal set function `f : d → r`. -/
def mapsTo {n} (f d r : Fin n) : Formula n :=
  rename ![f, d, r]
    ((functionOn 0 1).conj
      (Formula.all (Formula.all ((Formula.mem 3 1).imp ((edge 0 3 4).imp (Formula.mem 4 2))))) : Formula 3)

/-- Internal set surjection `f : d ↠ r`. -/
def surjection {n} (f d r : Fin n) : Formula n :=
  rename ![f, d, r]
    ((mapsTo 0 1 2).conj
      (Formula.all ((Formula.mem 3 2).imp (Formula.ex ((Formula.mem 4 1).conj (edge 0 4 3))))) : Formula 3)

/-- Internal set injection `f : d ↪ r`. -/
def injection {n} (f d r : Fin n) : Formula n :=
  rename ![f, d, r]
    ((mapsTo 0 1 2).conj
      (Formula.all (Formula.all (.all
        ((Formula.mem 3 1).imp ((Formula.mem 4 1).imp
          (((edge 0 3 5).conj (edge 0 4 5)).imp (Formula.equal 3 4))))))) : Formula 3)

/-- Transitivity of a set (not transitivity of the external structure). -/
def transitiveSet {n} (a : Fin n) : Formula n :=
  rename ![a]
    (Formula.all (Formula.all ((Formula.mem 1 0).imp ((Formula.mem 2 1).imp (Formula.mem 2 0)))) : Formula 1)

/-- Membership is an internal strict linear order on `a`. -/
def membershipLinear {n} (a : Fin n) : Formula n :=
  rename ![a]
    ((Formula.all ((Formula.mem 1 0).imp (Formula.mem 1 1).neg)).conj
      ((Formula.all (Formula.all (Formula.all ((Formula.mem 1 0).imp ((Formula.mem 2 0).imp ((Formula.mem 3 0).imp
        (((Formula.mem 1 2).conj (Formula.mem 2 3)).imp (Formula.mem 1 3)))))))).conj
        (Formula.all (Formula.all ((Formula.mem 1 0).imp ((Formula.mem 2 0).imp
          ((Formula.equal 1 2).disj ((Formula.mem 1 2).disj (Formula.mem 2 1)))))))) : Formula 1)

/-- Every nonempty internal subset of `a` has a membership-minimal element.
This does NOT assert external well-foundedness of the relation. -/
def membershipWellFounded {n} (a : Fin n) : Formula n :=
  rename ![a]
    (Formula.all (((subset 1 0).conj (nonempty 1)).imp
      (Formula.ex ((Formula.mem 2 1).conj (Formula.all ((Formula.mem 3 1).imp (Formula.mem 3 2).neg))))) : Formula 1)

/-- A von Neumann ordinal: a transitive set internally wellordered by membership. -/
def ordinal {n} (a : Fin n) : Formula n :=
  (transitiveSet a).conj ((membershipLinear a).conj (membershipWellFounded a))

/-- A choice function on the set `A` of nonempty sets. -/
def choiceOn {n} (c A : Fin n) : Formula n :=
  rename ![c, A]
    ((functionOn 0 1).conj
      (Formula.all ((Formula.mem 2 1).imp (Formula.ex ((edge 0 2 3).conj (Formula.mem 3 2))))) : Formula 2)

/-- A graph `F` coding a family of nonempty sets indexed by ordinal `η`. -/
def nonemptyFamily {n} (F η : Fin n) : Formula n :=
  rename ![F, η]
    ((functionOn 0 1).conj
      (Formula.all (Formula.all ((Formula.mem 2 1).imp ((edge 0 2 3).imp (nonempty 3))))) : Formula 2)

/-- `c(ξ) ∈ F(ξ)` for every `ξ ∈ η`, with `c` an internal function. -/
def choiceForFamily {n} (c η F : Fin n) : Formula n :=
  rename ![c, η, F]
    ((functionOn 0 1).conj
      (Formula.all (Formula.all ((Formula.mem 3 1).imp ((edge 2 3 4).imp
        (Formula.ex ((edge 0 3 5).conj (Formula.mem 5 4))))))) : Formula 3)

/-- The Partition Principle. The reverse injection is not required to be a
section of the original surjection. Both `X` and `Y` range over all sets. -/
def PP : Sentence :=
  Formula.all (Formula.all ((Formula.ex (surjection 2 0 1)).imp (Formula.ex (injection 2 1 0))))

/-- Ordinary Choice for every set of nonempty sets. -/
def AC : Sentence :=
  Formula.all ((Formula.all ((Formula.mem 1 0).imp (nonempty 1))).imp (Formula.ex (choiceOn 1 0)))

/-- Choice for every ordinal-indexed family of nonempty sets, including ordinals
which may fail to be well founded from outside a model. -/
def ACwo : Sentence :=
  Formula.all ((ordinal 0).imp
    (Formula.all ((nonemptyFamily 1 0).imp (Formula.ex (choiceForFamily 2 0 1)))))

/-! Full pure-set ZF, not a finite fragment and not ZFA. -/

def extensionality : Sentence :=
  Formula.all (Formula.all ((Formula.all ((Formula.mem 2 0).iff (Formula.mem 2 1))).imp (Formula.equal 0 1)))

def emptySet : Sentence := Formula.ex (empty 0)

def pairing : Sentence := Formula.all (Formula.all (Formula.ex (unorderedPair 2 0 1)))

def union : Sentence :=
  Formula.all (Formula.ex (Formula.all ((Formula.mem 2 1).iff (Formula.ex ((Formula.mem 3 0).conj (Formula.mem 2 3))))))

def powerSet : Sentence := Formula.all (Formula.ex (Formula.all ((Formula.mem 2 1).iff (subset 2 0))))

def infinity : Sentence :=
  Formula.ex ((Formula.ex ((Formula.mem 1 0).conj (empty 1))).conj
    (Formula.all ((Formula.mem 1 0).imp (Formula.ex ((Formula.mem 2 0).conj (successor 2 1))))))

def foundation : Sentence :=
  Formula.all ((nonempty 0).imp
    (Formula.ex ((Formula.mem 1 0).conj (Formula.all ((Formula.mem 2 1).imp (Formula.mem 2 0).neg)))))

/-- The instance of Separation for an entirely arbitrary formula `p(params,x)`.
All parameters are universally closed. Neither the quantifier complexity of
`p` nor the number of parameters is bounded. -/
def separation {n} (p : Formula (n + 1)) : Sentence :=
  let A : Fin (n + 3) := (Fin.last n).castSucc.castSucc
  let B : Fin (n + 3) := (Fin.last (n + 1)).castSucc
  let x : Fin (n + 3) := Fin.last (n + 2)
  let r : Fin (n + 1) → Fin (n + 3) :=
    Fin.lastCases x (fun i => i.castSucc.castSucc.castSucc)
  close (Formula.all (Formula.ex (Formula.all ((Formula.mem x B).iff ((Formula.mem x A).conj (rename r p))))))

/-- Substitution into a binary formula with `n` additional parameters. -/
def relation {n m} (p : Formula (n + 2)) (params : Fin n → Fin m) (x y : Fin m) :
    Formula m :=
  rename (Fin.lastCases y (Fin.lastCases x params)) p

/-- The instance of Replacement for an entirely arbitrary formula
`p(params,x,y)`: if it is single-valued and total on `A`, its image is a set.
As with Separation, the parameters are all universally closed. -/
def replacement {n} (p : Formula (n + 2)) : Sentence :=
  let unique : Formula (n + 3) :=
    Formula.all ((relation p (fun i => i.castSucc.castSucc.castSucc.castSucc)
      (Fin.last (n + 1)).castSucc.castSucc (Fin.last (n + 3))).imp
      (Formula.equal (Fin.last (n + 3)) (Fin.last (n + 2)).castSucc))
  let totalFunctional : Formula (n + 1) :=
    Formula.all ((Formula.mem (Fin.last (n + 1)) (Fin.last n).castSucc).imp
      (Formula.ex ((relation p (fun i => i.castSucc.castSucc.castSucc)
        (Fin.last (n + 1)).castSucc (Fin.last (n + 2))).conj unique)))
  let image : Formula (n + 1) :=
    Formula.ex (Formula.all ((Formula.mem (Fin.last (n + 2)) (Fin.last (n + 1)).castSucc).iff
      (Formula.ex ((Formula.mem (Fin.last (n + 3)) (Fin.last n).castSucc.castSucc.castSucc).conj
        (relation p (fun i => i.castSucc.castSucc.castSucc.castSucc)
          (Fin.last (n + 3)) (Fin.last (n + 2)).castSucc)))))
  close (Formula.all (totalFunctional.imp image))

/-- Exactly the axioms and all schema instances of pure first-order ZF. -/
inductive IsZFAxiom : Sentence → Prop where
  | extensionality : IsZFAxiom extensionality
  | emptySet : IsZFAxiom emptySet
  | pairing : IsZFAxiom pairing
  | union : IsZFAxiom union
  | powerSet : IsZFAxiom powerSet
  | infinity : IsZFAxiom infinity
  | foundation : IsZFAxiom foundation
  | separation {n} (p : Formula (n + 1)) : IsZFAxiom (separation p)
  | replacement {n} (p : Formula (n + 2)) : IsZFAxiom (replacement p)

/-- Zermelo–Fraenkel set theory, without Choice. -/
def ZF : Theory := {p | IsZFAxiom p}

/-- ZF with the Partition Principle, ordinal-indexed Choice and failure of Choice. -/
def targetTheory : Theory := ZF ∪ {PP, ACwo, AC.neg}

end SetLanguage

/-- Relative consistency of ZF with the Partition Principle, ordinal-indexed
Choice and failure of Choice. -/
theorem main_consistency :
    Consistent SetLanguage.ZF → Consistent SetLanguage.targetTheory := by
  sorry

end PartitionConsistency

end OAI

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that the Partition Principle does not imply the Axiom of Choice: if ZF is consistent, then so is ZF with the Partition Principle, Choice for ordinal-indexed families, and the negation of the Axiom of Choice. Separately, over every countable transitive model of ZFC, we construct a transitive symmetric model of this theory with the same ordinals and no new countable sequences of ground elements.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The *Partition Principle* asserts that a surjection between two sets implies the existence of an injection in the reverse direction: $$\mathsf{PP}:\qquad
 \forall X\,\forall Y\,
 \bigl[(\exists f:X\twoheadrightarrow Y)
       \Longrightarrow(\exists j:Y\hookrightarrow X)\bigr].$$ Equivalently, every partition of a set admits an injection into that set. The Axiom of Choice implies this principle by choosing one element of each fiber. The converse is less direct: the injection supplied by $\mathsf{PP}$ need not take a part to one of its members. In particular, it need not satisfy $f\circ j=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}_Y$. The problem is whether this cardinal comparison alone can recover choice for arbitrary families.

Write $\mathsf{AC}_{\mathrm{WO}}$ for Choice for wellorderable families: for every ordinal $\eta$ and every family $(X_\xi)_{\xi<\eta}$ of nonempty sets, there is a function $c$ with $c(\xi)\in X_\xi$ for all $\xi<\eta$. Our principal result is the following ordinary relative-consistency statement.

<a id="stmt-thm-main-consistency"></a>
<div id="thm:main-consistency" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *If $\mathsf{ZF}$ is consistent, then so is $\mathsf{ZF}+\mathsf{PP}+\mathsf{AC}_{\mathrm{WO}}+\neg\mathsf{AC}$.*

</div>

The construction also has a separate transitive-model form.

<a id="stmt-thm-main-transitive"></a>
<div id="thm:main-transitive" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2**. *Let $V$ be a countable transitive model of $\mathsf{ZFC}$. There are a set forcing $\mathbb Q\in V$, a $V$-generic filter $G$, and a transitive symmetric submodel $W$ of $V[G]$ such that $$V\subseteq W\subseteq V[G],\qquad
 \mathrm{Ord}^W=\mathrm{Ord}^V,\qquad
 W\models\mathsf{ZF}+\mathsf{PP}+\mathsf{AC}_{\mathrm{WO}}+\neg\mathsf{AC}.$$ Every countable sequence of elements of $V$ that belongs to $W$ already belongs to $V$.*

</div>

Here $\mathsf{ZF}$ is classical first-order set theory with pure sets, Foundation, and the full Separation and Replacement schemata. Theorem <a href="#thm:main-consistency" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main-consistency">1</a> gives a negative answer to the Partition Principle versus Choice problem under ordinary syntactic consistency. It permits externally ill-founded models, whereas Theorem <a href="#thm:main-transitive" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main-transitive">2</a> starts with a transitive ground and preserves transitivity. In either conclusion the reverse injections and the family without a choice function belong to the same model. Section <a href="#sec:metatheory" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:metatheory">8</a> gives the two deductions separately. The consequences for the dual Cantor–Schröder–Bernstein principle and the Weak Partition Principle are developed in Remark <a href="#rem:dual-csb-wpp" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rem:dual-csb-wpp">30</a>.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History and related methods

The distinction between comparing the sizes of a partition and its underlying set, and actually choosing representatives, is longstanding. Da Silva traces the Partition Principle to Beppo Levi in 1902 (Silva 2021, sec. 2, p. 787) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[daSilva2021](#ref-daSilva2021)]. Banaschewski and Moore studied its relation to the dual Cantor–Bernstein theorem and the surrounding cardinal-comparison principles (Banaschewski and Moore 1990) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[BanaschewskiMoore1990](#ref-BanaschewskiMoore1990)]. These questions concern what remains of cardinal arithmetic when sets need not be wellorderable. For example, Blass and Kulshreshtha distinguish several forms of cardinal well-foundedness and show that they become equivalent under $\mathsf{PP}$; their August 2026 version still lists $\mathsf{PP}\Rightarrow\mathsf{AC}$ as open (Blass and Kulshreshtha 2026, Introduction and Section 9) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[BlassKulshreshtha2026](#ref-BlassKulshreshtha2026)]. Gilson’s September 2026 corrections withdraw the model-construction claims of three earlier approaches (Gilson 2026c, 2026b, 2026a) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[GilsonSheafCorrection2026](#ref-GilsonSheafCorrection2026), [GilsonExternalCorrection2026](#ref-GilsonExternalCorrection2026), [GilsonIterationCorrection2026](#ref-GilsonIterationCorrection2026)].

There is already substantial choice in $\mathsf{PP}$. The implication $\mathsf{PP}\Rightarrow\mathsf{AC}_{\mathrm{WO}}$ is classical: Ryan-Smith records the argument attributed to Pincus by Pelc (Ryan-Smith 2025, Proposition 3.17, proof and footnote 4) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[RyanSmith2025](#ref-RyanSmith2025)]. Thus $\mathsf{AC}_{\mathrm{WO}}$ in our main statements is not an additional strength beyond $\mathsf{PP}$. Its separate appearance records the order of the construction: we establish ordinal-indexed choice first and then use it to assemble reverse injections for arbitrary sets.

The difficulty of preserving a principle quantified over all sets can be reduced to a fixed set of parameters. Blass introduced small violations of choice (Blass 1979) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Blass1979](#ref-Blass1979)]. For a nonempty set $A$, $\mathrm{SVC}(A)$ says that every nonempty set is a surjective image of $A\times\eta$ for some ordinal $\eta$. The injective version, $\mathrm{SVC}^{+}(A)$, says that every set embeds into some $A\times\eta$. See also (Usuba 2021, Definition 3.13 and Theorem 3.14) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Usuba2021](#ref-Usuba2021)]. These presentations let a construction on one set control objects of arbitrary rank, but their different directions cannot be interchanged without proof.

Ryan-Smith makes the local-to-global step precise. Under $\mathrm{SVC}^{+}(A)$, full $\mathsf{PP}$ is equivalent to $\mathsf{AC}_{\mathrm{WO}}$ together with reverse injections for surjections between subsets of $A$ (Ryan-Smith 2025, Proposition 3.17) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[RyanSmith2025](#ref-RyanSmith2025)]. Reverse injections for surjective images of $A$ are a different local assertion: even with $\mathsf{AC}_{\mathrm{WO}}$ and $\mathrm{SVC}^{+}(A)$, they need not imply full $\mathsf{PP}$ (Ryan-Smith 2025, Proposition 3.23) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[RyanSmith2025](#ref-RyanSmith2025)]. Our construction instead makes every nonwellorderable image of $A$ bijective to $A$. Lemma <a href="#lemma:pp-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:pp-reduction">28</a> uses this stronger classification, the surjective presentation, and ordinal-indexed choice to carry out the required all-set reduction. Its slicing argument follows the method of (Ryan-Smith 2025, Propositions 3.17 and 3.20) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[RyanSmith2025](#ref-RyanSmith2025)], rather than identifying the two local principles.

The ambient method is a symmetric submodel of a set-forcing extension: hereditarily symmetric names for a normal filter of subgroups give a model of full $\mathsf{ZF}$ (Karagila 2026a, sec. 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Karagila2026](#ref-Karagila2026)]. A more specific antecedent is Holy and Schilhan’s product of Cohen forcing with a forcing of ground-model hereditarily almost-disjoint towers (Holy and Schilhan 2025, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[HolySchilhan2025](#ref-HolySchilhan2025)]. Their construction yields models in which every set can be linearly ordered and higher forms of Dependent Choice hold, although full Choice fails. They develop the hereditary almost-disjointness method from Pincus’s work on adding Dependent Choice (Pincus 1977) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Pincus1977](#ref-Pincus1977)]. Their restriction and support-intersection arguments (Holy and Schilhan 2025, Lemmas 10 and 15) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[HolySchilhan2025](#ref-HolySchilhan2025)] are antecedents of the locality arguments here. Our diagrams have a different geometry: the monoid must support both exact quotient multiplicities and the coherent assignment of bijections, not just the descent of supports.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Proof strategy

We construct a set $A$ of distinct generic subsets of the ground-model $\omega_1$. Symmetry prevents $A$ from being wellordered. Orbit maps of names show that every nonempty set is a surjective image of $A\times\eta$ for some ordinal $\eta$, with no restriction on its rank. The forcing also allows one wellorderable part of $A$ to meet every member of any given ordinal-indexed family of nonempty subsets of $A$. Together with the preceding presentation of arbitrary sets, this yields $\mathsf{AC}_{\mathrm{WO}}$.

<a id="eq:intro-quotient"></a>
The central additional property is $$\label{eq:intro-quotient}
 \text{every nonwellorderable surjective image of $A$ is in bijection with $A$.}$$ To pass from this property to full $\mathsf{PP}$, partition the target of an arbitrary surjection into ordinal-indexed disjoint nonempty pieces and use disjoint source pieces mapping onto them. Each resulting piece is an image of $A$, though the source pieces need not cover the original domain. A wellorderable target piece admits a reverse injection by $\mathsf{AC}_{\mathrm{WO}}$. Otherwise both corresponding pieces are in bijection with $A$ by <a href="#eq:intro-quotient" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-quotient">[eq:intro-quotient]</a>. A further application of $\mathsf{AC}_{\mathrm{WO}}$ chooses the piecewise injections, whose union is the required injection. The empty target uses the empty injection.

The difficult part is to construct the bijections in <a href="#eq:intro-quotient" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-quotient">[eq:intro-quotient]</a> with enough coherence to belong to the symmetric model. Fix one equivalence relation on $A$ whose quotient is not wellorderable. Counting its classes on each support will produce many possible local bijections, but arbitrary choices of them need not agree on overlapping supports or respect coordinate changes. The proof solves these two compatibility problems in three stages.

1.  **A geometry of diagrams.** We build a left-cancellative monoid $T$. A diagram is an injective map $r:T\to I$, where $I$ is the set of label indices. Its subdiagram at $n\in T$ is the map $r_n(x)=r(nx)$. The diagram forcing makes larger diagrams stronger conditions. Finite amalgamations join independently placed diagrams. A second extension operation places many copies with prescribed patterns of intersection. Together these operations give the forcing its compatibility and support-descent properties. Height and cost functions reduce the availability patterns introduced next to countable cofinal paths. A rearrangement of the same monoid construction will later bound the symmetries that survive along such a path.

2.  **Exact availability fibers.** A diagram supports names whose definitions are fixed by all permutations of labels outside its range. For a quotient of $A$, we record the quotient elements that can be named at each diagram. An element’s availability pattern records the subdiagrams at which it can still be named. The labels themselves have analogous patterns, determined by which subdiagrams contain their indices. The copying and support-descent lemmas show that, for each exact pattern, the available labels and quotient elements have the same cardinality. Restricting to a subdiagram in that pattern identifies the corresponding fibers canonically. These identifications group the fibers into components and are already compatible; the choices of bijections between label and quotient fibers are not.

3.  **Covariant choices on each component.** Each component has a countable cofinal chain of nested diagrams. Codes along this chain give a preliminary bijection compatible with every permutation fixing a sufficiently late diagram pointwise. Modulo these permutations, the remaining symmetries form a countable group. Countably many bounded initial segments of Cohen subsets distinguish its elements and select among the transported preliminary bijections. Finally, transport between components gives assignments that agree under restriction and respect all required permutations. Their union is one hereditarily symmetric graph for the fixed quotient.

The countable description of each availability pattern, the reduction of its symmetries to a countable group, and the final equivariant selection are the main technical ingredients. The selection is performed between moved Cohen generics: it does not require permutations to act as automorphisms of a single fixed forcing extension.

Section <a href="#sec:monoid" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:monoid">2</a> constructs $T$ and proves its local rearrangement property. Section <a href="#sec:forcing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:forcing">3</a> develops the forcing, locality, and the basic properties of $A$. The quotient construction follows in three stages: Section <a href="#sec:quotient-fibers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:quotient-fibers">4</a> computes exact availability fibers, Section <a href="#sec:quotient-components" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:quotient-components">5</a> organizes them into components and controls their symmetries, and Section <a href="#sec:quotient-equivariance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:quotient-equivariance">6</a> constructs the coherent bijections. Section <a href="#sec:conclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conclusion">7</a> derives full $\mathsf{PP}$ and identifies a family without a choice function. Section <a href="#sec:metatheory" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:metatheory">8</a> gives the ordinary consistency deduction and the separate transitive-ground result.

<a id="sec:monoid"></a>
### A monoid of diagrams

The forcing construction will compare a diagram with its translated subdiagrams. We first construct the monoid that indexes these translations. Its two extension operations serve different purposes: one realizes finitely many prescribed overlaps, and the other inserts many new positions with a prescribed set of old divisors. A further property of the construction will control symmetries along countable systems of subdiagrams.

All constructions in this section take place in a universe satisfying $\mathsf{ZFC}$. No inaccessible cardinal is used. Fix an infinite cardinal $\mu\geq\aleph_1$ such that $\mu^{\aleph_0}=\mu$.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### The required algebra and the construction theorem

Let $M$ be a monoid with identity $1$. Write $x\sqsubseteq y$ if $xz=y$ for some $z$. We use only left cancellation. A monoid is *conical* if $xy=1$ implies $x=y=1$. Left cancellation and conicality make $\sqsubseteq$ a partial order and make the right factor in a comparison unique. Write $x\sqsubset y$ for $x\sqsubseteq y$ and $x\ne y$, and $\downarrow a=\{x:x\sqsubseteq a\}$. A *conditional join* is an element $x\vee y$ satisfying $$xM\cap yM=(x\vee y)M$$ whenever the intersection is nonempty. Thus a join is required exactly when the two elements have a common right multiple. An *ideal* in $\downarrow a$ will mean a nonempty downward closed subset closed under finite nonempty joins. These joins exist because $a$ is an upper bound.

An inclusion $B\subseteq M$ is *conservative* if it preserves and reflects comparisons, preserves their right factors, preserves existing joins, and preserves the absence of a common right multiple. In particular, if $x,y\in B$ and $xz=y$ in $M$, then $z\in B$ and the same equation holds in $B$. Mere injectivity is not this property.

<a id="eq:mono-margin"></a>
We shall also keep real-valued height and cost functions $\rho,\epsilon$ on $M$, writing $\epsilon_x=\epsilon(x)$, and satisfying $$\label{eq:mono-margin}
 \begin{gathered}
 0<\rho(x)<1,\qquad 0<\epsilon_x\leq1,\\
 \rho(y)-\rho(x)>
 \sup\{\epsilon_d:d\sqsubseteq y,\ d\not\sqsubseteq x\}
 \qquad(x\sqsubset y).
 \end{gathered}$$ An empty supremum of costs is understood to be zero.

<a id="stmt-lem-mono-countable-ideal"></a>
<div id="lem:mono-countable-ideal" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3**. *If a monoid has conditional joins and satisfies Equation <a href="#eq:mono-margin" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mono-margin">[eq:mono-margin]</a>, every ideal in $\downarrow a$ has a nondecreasing cofinal sequence of length $\omega$, with repetitions allowed. The same conclusion holds in any $\mathsf{ZFC}$ extension in which the monoid, order, heights and costs retain their meanings, for ideals belonging to that extension.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-mono-countable-ideal"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-mono-countable-ideal</summary>

*Proof.* For an ideal $U$, choose elements whose heights approach $\sup\rho[U]$, and replace their successive initial segments by their finite joins. This gives a nondecreasing sequence $(n_i)$ in $U$ with heights approaching the same supremum. If $d\in U$ divides none of the $n_i$, then $$\rho(n_i\vee d)-\rho(n_i)>\epsilon_d$$ for every $i$, contradicting convergence to the supremum. Prepending $1$ makes $n_0=1$. The proof applies in the stated extension as well; its choices are made in that $\mathsf{ZFC}$ universe. ◻

</details>

Here is the finite overlap condition used below. Take finitely many copies $\{i\}\times M$. For each pair of distinct copies prescribe either no overlap or elements $a_{ij},a_{ji}\in M$ and the identifications $$(i,a_{ij}x)\sim(j,a_{ji}x)\qquad(x\in M).$$ We call the prescription *valid* if these pair identifications, together with equality, already form an equivalence relation and do not identify distinct elements of any one copy. In particular, transitive closure is not allowed to create extra overlaps.

<a id="stmt-thm-monoid"></a>
<div id="thm:monoid" class="theorem">

**Theorem 4**. *There is a monoid $T$ of cardinality $\mu$, with a distinguished $e\ne1$, having the following properties.*

1.  *$T$ is left cancellative and conical, has conditional joins, and has heights and costs satisfying Equation <a href="#eq:mono-margin" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mono-margin">[eq:mono-margin]</a>.*

2.  *Every valid finite overlap prescription on copies of $T$ is realized by left translations $x\mapsto l_i x$ in $T$, with exactly the prescribed identifications.*

<a id="eq:mono-probes"></a>
3.  *For $a,b\in T$ with $b\ne1$, and every ideal $U\subseteq\downarrow a$, there are $\mu$ distinct elements $m$ with $$\label{eq:mono-probes}
     \begin{gathered}
     mb=ae,\qquad m\not\sqsubseteq a,\qquad
     \downarrow m\cap\downarrow a=U,\\
     n\vee m=ae\quad(n\sqsubseteq a,\ n\notin U).
     \end{gathered}$$ The cones of distinct elements in this family intersect exactly in $aeT$. Moreover $|T\setminus eT|=\mu$.*

4.  *For every countable subset of $T$ there is a countable submonoid $T_0$ containing it and a continuous conservative tower starting at $T_0$ and ending at $T$. Each successor step has one of the following properties:*

    1.  *no new element divides an old element;*

<a id="eq:mono-gate-old"></a>
    2.  *every new element $w$ with an old right multiple has a least old right multiple $g(w)$, and $$\label{eq:mono-gate-old}
         d\text{ old},\quad g(w)\sqsubseteq wd
               \quad\Longrightarrow\quad wd\text{ old}.$$*

    *Here “old” means belonging to the base of that particular successor step, and the comparisons may be tested in the final monoid.*

</div>

For diagrams indexed by $T$, the finite-merger clause supplies a common larger diagram for finitely many copies with compatible overlaps. The probe clause supplies $\mu$ copies with one prescribed intersection pattern: for $n\sqsubseteq a$, the cone $nT$ contains $mT$ exactly when $n\in U$, and otherwise meets it in $aeT$. The margin makes every such ideal countably cofinal.
